# Metric descent and rank-preserving contractions

Coordinate `EN-103-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-M-0161` | `PM-068.5` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Metric-descent-and-rank-preserving-contractions-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Metric-descent-and-rank-preserving-contractions-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "From a smooth semipositive anticanonical metric on a smooth projective complex variety, we construct an unweighted integrable semipositive metric on a corrected anticanonical line of a smooth base, using a normal equidimensional toroidal model and full generic adjoint rank one at exponent zero. The explicit rational boundary correction pulls back to an exceptional divisor. On varieties without positive-degree holomorphic forms, two-metric transfer gives sections with prescribed boundary poles from a finite-volume log-anticanonical metric and a target line with bounded semipositive weights. In particular, a smooth projective complex variety with a smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle and no such forms has a nonzero invariant section of a positive anticanonical multiple for every torus action with its natural linearization. On this class of varieties, generic section and adjoint-rank hypotheses give contractions of invariant fibrations that preserve both conditions unless an invariant global section already exists.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> From a smooth semipositive anticanonical metric on a smooth projective complex variety, we construct an unweighted integrable semipositive metric on a corrected anticanonical line of a smooth base, using a normal equidimensional toroidal model and full generic adjoint rank one at exponent zero. The explicit rational boundary correction pulls back to an exceptional divisor. On varieties without positive-degree holomorphic forms, two-metric transfer gives sections with prescribed boundary poles from a finite-volume log-anticanonical metric and a target line with bounded semipositive weights. In particular, a smooth projective complex variety with a smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle and no such forms has a nonzero invariant section of a positive anticanonical multiple for every torus action with its natural linearization. On this class of varieties, generic section and adjoint-rank hypotheses give contractions of invariant fibrations that preserve both conditions unless an invariant global section already exists.

- **claim-toroidal-volume-metric** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-toroidal-volume)): Let $X$ and $Y$ be smooth connected projective complex varieties, with $n=\dim X$ and $d=\dim Y>0$, and let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth Hermitian metric $h$ of semipositive curvature $\alpha$. Suppose $$p:W\longrightarrow X,\qquad f:W\longrightarrow Y$$ are projective morphisms, where $W$ is normal and integral, $p$ is birational, and $f$ is surjective, equidimensional, and toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings. Choose a smooth projective resolution $\rho:Z\to W$, and put $\pi=p\rho$, $g=f\rho$, and $E_Z=K_{Z/X}$. Assume $$\label{g:volume-rank-zero} \operatorname{rk}g_*\mathcal O_Z(E_Z)=1.$$ For each prime divisor $Q\subset Y$ and each prime $E\subset W$ dominating $Q$, set $$a_E=\operatorname{ord}_E K_{W/X},\qquad e_E=\operatorname{ord}_E f^*Q,$$ where the Jacobian divisor $K_{W/X}=K_W-p^*K_X$ is defined in codimension one. Define $$\label{g:volume-correction} a_Q=\max\left\{0,\min_{f(E)=Q} \left(\frac{1+a_E}{e_E}-1\right)\right\}, \qquad A_0=\sum_Q a_Q Q.$$ Then $A_0$ is an effective rational divisor with finite support, and $f^*A_0$ is $p$-exceptional. The rational line bundle $-K_Y+A_0$ has a singular Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature whose squared frame norms are locally integrable without a divisor weight. This metric is smooth on a nonempty Zariski open subset of $Y$.* *Moreover, let $U\subset Y$ be a nonempty Zariski open set where $g$ is smooth and formation of the adjoint direct image $g_*(K_{Z/Y}+\pi^*L)$ commutes with base change. If there are a nonempty ordinary open patch $\mathcal V\subset g^{-1}(U)\setminus\operatorname{Supp}E_Z$, a Kähler form $\omega_Y$, and a constant $c>0$ such that $$\label{g:volume-horizontal-patch} \pi^*\alpha\geq c\,g^*\omega_Y\qquad\text{on }\mathcal V,$$ then the constructed metric is strictly positively curved on a nonempty ordinary open subset of $Y$.
  - Explanation: Pushing forward the anticanonical volume along a toroidal equidimensional model gives an unweighted integrable semipositive metric on the corrected base anticanonical line, with exceptional pullback of the correction.
- **claim-volume-subklt** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-volume-subklt)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties with $\dim Y>0$, let $W$ be normal and integral, and let $p:W\to X$ be projective birational and $f:W\to Y$ projective, surjective, equidimensional, and toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings. Define $A_0$ by <a href="#g:volume-correction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:volume-correction">[g:volume-correction]</a>, and put $$\Theta=-K_{W/X}+f^*A_0.$$ Then $\Theta\leq0$, the pair $(W,\Theta)$ is sub-klt, and $f^*A_0$ is $p$-exceptional. In particular, for every smooth model $\tau:Z'\to W$, $$\label{g:volume-all-valuations} \operatorname{coeff}_F\bigl(K_{Z'/X}-(f\tau)^*A_0\bigr)>-1$$ at every prime $F\subset Z'$.
  - Explanation: The boundary built from Jacobian and correction is nonpositive and sub-klt, so every valuation has discrepancy above the integrability threshold.
- **claim-correction-iitaka-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-correction-iitaka-zero)): For the correction in Theorem <a href="#g:toroidal-volume" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:toroidal-volume">1</a>, $\kappa(Y,A_0)=0$. More precisely, for every positive integer $m$ for which $mA_0$ is integral, $$H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y(mA_0)) =\mathbb C\,s_{mA_0}.$$
  - Explanation: Sections of multiples of the correction have only exceptional poles upstairs, hence are constant.
- **claim-two-metric-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-rb-transfer)): Let $V$ be smooth connected projective, let $D\geq0$ be integral, and suppose $A=-K_V+D$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_A$ with $\int_V\|s_D\|_{h_A}^2<\infty$. Let $L$ have a semipositive metric $h_L$ with bounded weights. Use the model diagram above with $0<\dim Y\leq\dim V$. Suppose a nonzero rational section $\rho$ of $kL$, $k>0$, has no horizontal poles on $\bar V$. On a dense base open set put $$g_0(y)\,d\lambda_y=f_*p^*\mu, \qquad S_\rho(y)=\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{W_y}\|p^*\rho\|_{h_L^k}^2.$$ Assume $-\log g_0$ in coordinate frames and $-\log S_\rho$ have psh representatives on this open set. Then, after clearing a positive integer denominator, there are a line bundle $B$ with bounded semipositive weights, an effective integral divisor $D_Y$, and a regular nonzero map $$f^*B\longrightarrow bk\,p^*L$$ for some $b>0$. The line bundle $-K_Y+D_Y$ has a semipositive metric whose distinguished adjoint measure has finite mass. Every nonzero section of $aB+cD_Y$, where $a>0$ and $c\geq0$ are integers, produces a nonzero rational section of $abkL$ with poles only on $D$. If the data and $\rho$ are invariant for a torus acting trivially on $Y$, then the produced section is invariant.
  - Explanation: A finite-volume log-anticanonical metric and a bounded target metric transfer to a lower-dimensional base, returning sections with poles only on the original boundary.
- **claim-rank-one-sufficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-rank-one)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#rb:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rb:transfer">4</a>, suppose on the generic fibre of $f$ that $$h^0\bigl(W_\eta,(J+p^*D+mkp^*L)\|_{W_\eta}\bigr)=1 \qquad(m\geq0).$$ Then the two psh assumptions in that lemma hold. There is also an invariant version: take $D=0$, smooth input metrics invariant under a compact form of an acting torus, and an invariant $\rho$; it suffices that the invariant dimensions in the display equal one.
  - Explanation: Generic fibre adjoint spaces of dimension one give the plurisubharmonic inputs of the transfer lemma, also invariantly.
- **claim-determinant-divisors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-determinant)): Let $L$ have bounded semipositive weights on a smooth projective $n$-fold $V$ with $H^0(V,\Omega_V^j)=0$ for $j>0$. There are a line bundle $M$ mapping nontrivially into a tensor power of $\Omega_V^1$ and effective integral divisors $$N_i\sim M+m_iL, \qquad m_i\longrightarrow+\infty.$$ The integers $m_i$ can be chosen strictly increasing and divisible by any prescribed positive integer.
  - Explanation: Twisted forms and a saturated determinant give effective divisors in unbounded twists of the bounded line.
- **claim-rank-preserving-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-contraction-theorem)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety with $H^0(X,\Omega_X^q)=0$ for $q>0$, and suppose that $L=-K_X$ has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$, with its natural action on $L$. Let $f:X\dashrightarrow Z$ be a dominant $T$-invariant rational map to a smooth connected projective variety, where $\mathbb C(Z)$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$. On a smooth projective resolution $\pi:Y\to X$, $f_Y:Y\to Z$, put $L_Y=\pi^*L$, $E_Y=K_{Y/X}$, and $\eta=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C(Z)$. Suppose that:* 1. *$H^0(Y_\eta,pL_Y\|_{Y_\eta})^T\ne0$ for some integer $p>0$;* 2. *there is an integer $a>0$ such that $$\dim_{\mathbb C(Z)}H^0(Y_\eta,(E_Y+mL_Y)\|_{Y_\eta})^T=1 \quad\text{for every positive multiple }m\text{ of }a.$$* *Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or there is a dominant rational map $h:Z\dashrightarrow W$ to a smooth connected projective variety with $\dim W<\dim Z$ such that $h\circ f$ satisfies the same two conditions, possibly with different positive integers. The field $\mathbb C(W)$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$.
  - Explanation: Either a global invariant multiple section exists or the fibration contracts to a lower-dimensional base preserving both generic conditions.
- **claim-toroidal-density-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-cf-density-bounds)): For the prepared toroidal diagram and smooth positive volume above, near an arbitrary base point, with $y_i=0$ the local boundary branches, for every $\eta>0$, $$\label{g:cf:upper-density} \rho(y)\leq C_\eta \prod_i\|y_i\|^{-2(1-c_i+\eta)}$$ on the general smooth locus. Near a general point of $P_i$, $$\label{g:cf:lower-density} \rho(y)\geq C\|y_i\|^{-2(1-c_i)},\qquad C>0.$$ At a general point of a prime outside the base boundary, $\rho$ also has a positive constant lower bound.
  - Explanation: Two-sided local power bounds for the pushforward density along the toroidal boundary.
- **claim-contraction-metric-conclusions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-metric-conclusions)): In the preceding setup, $V$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. For the effective rational divisors $B^+$ and $B^-$ defined in <a href="#g:cf:thresholds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:cf:thresholds">[g:cf:thresholds]</a>, there is a nef rational divisor $M$ such that $$\label{g:cf:log-relation} K_Z+B^++M\sim_{\mathbb Q}B^-.$$ The support of $B^+$ has simple normal crossings and all its coefficients are less than one. The divisor $B^-$ is supported on those primes of $Z$ all of whose components on $Y_0$ are exceptional over $X$; in particular it is supported on $G$.
  - Explanation: A bounded metric on the base divisor and a nef divisor relating canonical class and signed boundary.
- **claim-cotangent-slope-supporting-class** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-cf-cotangent-slope)): Every line subsheaf $N\subseteq(\Omega_Z^1)^{\otimes s}$, for $s\geq0$, satisfies $c_1(N)\cdot\beta\leq0$.
  - Explanation: Cotangent tensor lines have nonpositive degree against the supporting movable class.
- **claim-effective-divisor-sequence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-effective-sequence)): There are a fixed line bundle $N$ and effective divisors $D_j$ such that $$\label{g:cf:divisor-sequence} D_j\sim m_jV+N,\qquad m_j\longrightarrow+\infty,\qquad D_j\cdot\beta=0,$$ where the positive integers $m_j$ may be chosen strictly increasing and sufficiently divisible.
  - Explanation: A fixed line and unbounded multiples of the base divisor give effective divisors of degree zero against the class.
- **claim-maximal-field-ratios** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-maximal-field)): Every ratio of two nonzero sections in any one linear system whose divisor class has degree zero against $\beta$ belongs to $\mathbb C(W)\subseteq\mathbb C(Z)$.
  - Explanation: Ratios of sections of zero-degree systems lie in the Stein base function field of a maximal system.
- **claim-horizontal-effectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-horizontal-slope)): The rational divisor $h^*V$ is rationally effective on the complete generic fiber of $\widehat g$.
  - Explanation: Affine horizontal multiplicities give nonnegative slopes, so the pulled-back divisor is rationally effective on the generic fibre.
- **claim-weighted-subklt-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-bt-weighted-approximation)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective, of dimension $d>0$, let $J$ be a rational line bundle with a semipositive metric of weights $\psi$, and let $P\geq0$ be a rational divisor. Suppose that, in local divisor frames, $$\|s_P\|^2e^{-\psi}\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}.$$ There is a fixed ample Cartier divisor $A$ such that, for every sufficiently large divisible integer $m$, there is an effective rational divisor $\Delta_m\sim_{\mathbb Q}J+A/m$ for which $(Y,\Delta_m-P)$ is sub-klt. When $P=0$, the pair $(Y,\Delta_m)$ is klt.
  - Explanation: Multiplier ideals with a fixed ample twist give effective divisors whose twisted pair is sub-klt.
- **claim-foliation-to-tensor-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-bt-slope-principle)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective and $P\geq0$ rational. Suppose that $-K_Y+P$ is pseudoeffective and that $K_{\mathcal F}-K_Y+P$ is pseudoeffective for every algebraically integrable saturated foliation $\mathcal F\subset T_Y$ of positive rank and corank. Then $hP-c_1(M)$ is pseudoeffective for every line subsheaf $M\subseteq(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes h}$, $h\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Pseudoeffective foliation determinants force pseudoeffectivity of the boundary twist minus any cotangent line.
- **claim-finite-volume-cotangent** (bound, [source](#stmt-rb-cotangent)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective and $P\geq0$ rational. If $J=-K_Y+P$ has a semipositive metric with $\|s_P\|^2e^{-\psi}$ locally integrable, then $hP-c_1(M)$ is pseudoeffective for every line subsheaf $M\subseteq(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes h}$. If $\dim Y>0$ and $\alpha\ne0$ is movable, then $\mu_{\alpha,\min}(T_Y)\geq-P\cdot\alpha$. In particular, $P\cdot\alpha=0$ gives $\mu_{\alpha,\min}(T_Y)\geq0$ and $c_1(M)\cdot\alpha\leq0$.
  - Explanation: A locally integrable weighted metric bounds cotangent lines and the minimal tangent slope against movable classes.
- **claim-unweighted-movable-slope** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-bt-unweighted-slope)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective of dimension $d>0$, and let $J$ have a semipositive metric with $e^{-\psi}\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$. Suppose $K_Y+J$ is pseudoeffective. For every movable class $\alpha$ and every line subsheaf $M\subseteq(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes h}$, $h>0$, $$\label{g:bt:orbifold-bound} c_1(M)\cdot\alpha\leq h d^{h-1}(K_Y+J)\cdot\alpha.$$ Thus $\alpha\cdot(K_Y+J)=0$ gives $c_1(M)\cdot\alpha\leq0$. For $h=0$ the latter inequality holds without the annihilation premise.
  - Explanation: An unweighted integrable metric gives cotangent bounds against movable classes via orbifold tensor positivity.
- **claim-normalized-maximum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-bt-bounded-maximum)): In the normalization <a href="#g:bt:normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:normalization">[g:bt:normalization]</a>, suppose the negative logarithms of the adjoint moments are psh for all sufficiently large exponents on a smooth connected-fibre open set. Then the maximum norm gives a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights on $N$.
  - Explanation: Power-mean limits of moments give a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights on the normalized line.
- **claim-weighted-exceptional-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-bt-weighted-transfer)): Under <a href="#g:bt:all-exceptional-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:all-exceptional-rank">[g:bt:all-exceptional-rank]</a>, $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. For some integer $c\geq0$, $-K_Y+cB$ has a semipositive metric satisfying $\|s_B\|^{2c}e^{-\psi}\in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$. A nonzero section of any positive divisible multiple of $j(N+R)+lB$, with $j>0$ and $l\geq0$, gives a nonzero invariant section of a positive multiple of $L$ on $X$.
  - Explanation: Under the strong rank condition, bounded metric on N and weighted integrable metric on the corrected line, with sections returning to X.
- **claim-integrable-toroidal-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-bt-toroidal-transfer)): Use the smooth metric and action above. Let $p:W\to X$ be birational with $W$ normal, and let $f:W\to Y$ be projective, equidimensional and toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings, with $Y$ smooth. Include the exceptional locus of $p$ in the source boundary. Suppose that an invariant rational section $s$ of $aL$, $a>0$, is regular on the generic fibre and that on a smooth equivariant resolved diagram $$\label{g:bt:zeroth-rank} \dim_K H^0\bigl(F,(K_{Z/X}+ma\pi^*L)\|_F\bigr)^T=1 \qquad(m\geq0).$$ Normalize $s$ by vertical minima as in <a href="#g:bt:normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:normalization">[g:bt:normalization]</a>. For a boundary prime $Q$ put $$A_E=1+\operatorname{ord}_E K_{W/X},\qquad c_Q=\min_{E\mapsto Q}\frac{A_E}{e_E},\qquad \Theta=\sum_Q\max\{c_Q-1,0\}Q.$$ Then $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights, and $-K_Y+\Theta$ has a semipositive metric with locally integrable squared frame norms. The pullback $f^*\Theta$ is exceptional over $X$.
  - Explanation: On a toroidal model the corrected base line carries an unweighted integrable metric with exceptional pullback.
- **claim-large-moment** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-bt-large-moment)): There is an integer $k$ in the range of <a href="#g:bt:eventual-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:eventual-rank">[g:bt:eventual-rank]</a> such that $$\Xi=K_{W/X}+kD-f^*R\geq0.$$ The rational line $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights, and $J=-K_Y+kN+R$ has a semipositive metric with $\mathcal I(\psi)=\mathcal O_Y$. A nonzero section of a positive divisible multiple of $N+R/k$ returns to a nonzero invariant section of a positive multiple of $L$ on $X$.
  - Explanation: One large moment gives a donor line with trivial multiplier ideal when rank one holds only eventually.
- **claim-signed-boundary-slope** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-bt-signed-slope)): Let $Y$ be smooth projective and let $B$ be a rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients less than one. Write $B=B^+-B^-$ in disjoint positive and negative parts, and suppose that $-(K_Y+B)$ is nef. Then $$hB^- -c_1(M) \quad\hbox{is pseudoeffective}$$ for every line subsheaf $M\subseteq(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes h}$, $h\geq0$. If $\dim Y>0$ and $\alpha\ne0$ is a movable class with $\alpha\cdot B^-=0$, this gives $\mu_{\alpha,\min}(T_Y)\geq0$ and $c_1(M)\cdot\alpha\leq0$.
  - Explanation: A nef negative log-canonical class with signed boundary bounds cotangent lines by the negative part.
- **claim-allowed-pole-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-allowed)): Let $V$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ with $H^0(V,\Omega_V^j)=0$ for every $j>0$. Let $D\geq0$ be integral and suppose $-K_V+D$ carries a semipositive singular metric whose measure $\|s_D\|^2$ has finite mass. If a line bundle $L$ carries a semipositive metric with bounded weights, then $$H^0(V,aL+cD)\ne0$$ for some integers $a>0$ and $c\geq0$. The same statement holds for a rational line bundle $L$, with $a$ clearing its denominator.
  - Explanation: A finite-volume log-anticanonical metric and a bounded target line give a nonzero section of a multiple twisted by the boundary.
- **claim-rc-fibre-space-method** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rb-rc-method)): The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#rb:allowed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rb:allowed">23</a> on a rationally connected $V$ follows by dimension induction through rationally connected bases, using fibre spaces that satisfy the following two conditions: for an ample divisor $H$ on the base, $$aL+cD-p_*f^*H\ \hbox{is pseudoeffective for some }a>0,c\geq0,$$ and a positive multiple of $p^*L$ has a nonzero section on the generic fibre without additional $D$-poles.
  - Explanation: Rationally connected case by induction through bases using maximal fibre spaces with two properties.
- **claim-invariant-anticanonical-section** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-invariant)): Let $V$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ with $H^0(V,\Omega_V^j)=0$ for all $j>0$. Suppose $-K_V$ has a smooth Hermitian metric of semipositive curvature. For any algebraic torus $T$ acting on $V$, there is an integer $m>0$ such that $$H^0(V,-mK_V)^T\ne0$$ for the natural anticanonical action.
  - Explanation: Nonzero torus-invariant section of a positive anticanonical multiple for the natural action.
- **claim-prekopa-integral-supremum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-prekopa)): Let $r\geq0$ be an integer, let $U$ be a complex manifold, and let $\Phi(z,x)$ be a smooth function on $U\times\mathbb R^r$ such that $\Phi(z,\operatorname{Re}w)$ is plurisubharmonic on $U\times\mathbb C^r$. Suppose $$0<\int_{\mathbb R^r}e^{-\Phi(z,x)}\,dx<\infty$$ for almost every $z$. Then the almost-everywhere functions $$-\log\int_{\mathbb R^r}e^{-\Phi(z,x)}\,dx, \qquad -\log\sup_{x\in\mathbb R^r}e^{-\Phi(z,x)}$$ have psh representatives, whenever the second expression is taken on the locus where its supremum is finite and positive. Under the stated integrability assumption that supremum is finite and positive for almost every $z$.
  - Explanation: Fibre integral and fibre supremum weights have plurisubharmonic representatives.
- **claim-uniqueness-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-uniqueness)): With these data, the following statements are equivalent.* 1. *For every integer $j\geq0$ and every nonzero invariant rational section $u$ of $jaL$ with no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$, one has $u/s^j\in K$.* 2. *For every integer $b\geq0$, the ratio of any two nonzero invariant rational sections of $bL$ having no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$ belongs to $K$.* *Both statements include degree zero.
  - Explanation: Two uniqueness conditions tested at original horizontal primes are equivalent.
- **claim-null-prime-field** (classification, [source](#stmt-g-cf-null-field)): Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $d>0$ and let $\alpha\ne0$ be a movable numerical curve class. Call a prime divisor $P$ *null* if $\alpha\cdot P=0$, and set $$K_\alpha=\{0\}\cup\{u\in\mathbb C(Y)^*: \operatorname{Supp}(\operatorname{div}u) \text{ consists of null primes}\}.$$ Then $K_\alpha$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(Y)$ and has transcendence degree less than $d$. It is the field of the Stein base of any linear system of degree zero against $\alpha$ having maximal image dimension among all such systems. Every prime of $Y$ vertical over $K_\alpha$ is null. If $\operatorname{Pic}(Y)$ is finitely generated, only finitely many null primes are horizontal over $K_\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Functions supported on null primes form a relatively algebraically closed field equal to the maximal-system Stein base field.
- **claim-finite-volume-base** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-cf-finite-volume-base)): The rational divisor $D$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. There is an effective integral divisor $P_0$, supported on the omitted primes, and a psh metric weight $\phi$ on $-K_Y+P_0$ such that $\|s_{P_0}\|^2e^{-\phi}$ is locally integrable.
  - Explanation: Bounded metric on the minimum divisor and an integrable log-anticanonical metric with correction on omitted primes.
- **claim-supporting-movable-class** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-supporting-class)): Assume the data of Section <a href="#g:cf:minima" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:minima">8.3</a> and suppose no positive power of $L$ has a nonzero invariant global section. Then there is a nonzero movable class $\alpha$ on $Y$ satisfying $$\alpha\cdot D=\alpha\cdot D^*=0, \qquad \alpha\cdot\Gamma=0 \quad\text{for every omitted prime }\Gamma.$$ In particular, $d^*_\Gamma=d_\Gamma$ whenever $\alpha\cdot\Gamma>0$.
  - Explanation: Cone duality gives a movable class annihilating the minimum divisors and omitted primes.
- **claim-finite-support-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-dickson)): Under the data of Section <a href="#g:cf:minima" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:minima">8.3</a>, assume no positive multiple of $L$ has an invariant global section. There are a positive integer $a'$ and an invariant rational section $s'$ of $a'L$ satisfying both the generic regularity condition and either uniqueness condition of Lemma <a href="#g:cf:uniqueness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:uniqueness">27</a> over $K_\alpha$. Moreover $\operatorname{trdeg}K_\alpha<\operatorname{trdeg}K$.
  - Explanation: Dickson's lemma on finite horizontal support yields a new regular section over a smaller field with uniqueness preserved.
- **claim-toric-initial-section** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-toric-initial-section)): Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on a smooth connected projective variety $X$, and suppose that $L=-K_X$ is nef. On an equivariant smooth projective resolution $\pi:Z\to X$, $g:Z\to Y$ of the rational quotient $\mathbb C(Y)=\mathbb C(X)^T$, some positive power of $\pi^*L$ has a nonzero invariant section on the complete generic fiber, for its natural linearization.
  - Explanation: Toric generation and weights of both signs give an invariant section on the generic fibre of the quotient.
- **claim-evaluation-slope** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-cf-evaluation-slope)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. Suppose that $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$ is dominant and invariant and that every invariant rational function on $X$ having no poles at the $f$-horizontal prime divisors of $X$ belongs to $\mathbb C(Y)$. Choose a normal projective graph model $$X\xleftarrow{p}W\xrightarrow{\varphi}Y$$ on which every vertical prime maps onto a prime divisor of $Y$. Let $N$ be the reduced sum of the primes of $Y$ above which all prime divisors of $W$ are exceptional over $X$.* *For every dominant rational map $g:Y\dashrightarrow U$ to a smooth projective variety, let $\mathcal F\subset T_Y$ be the saturated sheaf tangent to its general fibers. There is an integer $c\geq0$ such that $$\det(T_Y/\mathcal F)+cN \quad\text{is pseudoeffective}.$$ Consequently, if $\alpha\ne0$ is a movable curve class on $Y$ with $\alpha\cdot N=0$, then every line subsheaf $A\subset(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes j}$, $j\geq0$, satisfies $\alpha\cdot c_1(A)\leq0$.
  - Explanation: An evaluation metric makes the relative tangent determinant pseudoeffective up to omitted primes, bounding cotangent lines.
- **claim-four-generator-semigroup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-finite-semigroup)): Keep the fixed-section data of Section <a href="#g:cf:minima" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:minima">8.3</a> and assume $H^0(Y,\Omega_Y^q)=0$ for $q>0$. After taking a power of $s$, let $D,D^*$ be integral, put $G=D^*-D\geq0$, and let $N$ be the reduced omitted-prime divisor. Suppose $D$ has a bounded semipositive metric and that every cotangent line has nonpositive degree against every movable class annihilating $N$. If there is no invariant global section of a positive multiple of $L$, a smaller base preserving an invariant generic section of a positive multiple of $L$ and the original-prime uniqueness condition is obtained from one of the systems $$\|mD+iM+kG+lN\|,\qquad m,i,k,l\in\mathbb Z_{\geq0},$$ where $M$ is a single cotangent determinant line and $H^0(Y,M+m_jD)\ne0$ for unbounded positive $m_j$.
  - Explanation: A contraction preserving section and uniqueness comes from a maximal system in a four-generator semigroup.
- **claim-parameter-field-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-cf-parameter-contraction)): Assume fixed-section data $(f:X\dashrightarrow Y,k,s)$ as in Section <a href="#g:cf:conditions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:conditions">8.1</a>. Let $B,B^\sharp$ be the integral vertical-minimum divisors of the preceding normal equidimensional graph, and let $C_i$ be its omitted primes. Assume that an integral divisor $R$ and an unbounded sequence of effective divisors satisfy <a href="#g:cf:parameter-effective" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:cf:parameter-effective">[g:cf:parameter-effective]</a>. Then either a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section on $X$, or there is a dominant rational map $Y\dashrightarrow Y'$, with $\dim Y'<\dim Y$, such that its composite with $f$ admits fixed-section data satisfying the regularity and uniqueness conditions of Section <a href="#g:cf:conditions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:cf:conditions">8.1</a>. Its base field is relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$.
  - Explanation: A parameter field allowing poles at omitted primes gives a global section or a smaller base with fixed-section data.
- **claim-iitaka-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-rigidity)): Let $Q\geq0$ be an integral divisor on a smooth projective variety $Y$, and let $u:Y'\to Y$, $h:Y'\to Y_2$ resolve its Iitaka fibration, with connected generic fibre $S$. For every effective $u$-exceptional integral divisor $A$ and every positive integer $m$, $$h^0\bigl(S,m(u^*Q+A)\|_S\bigr)=1.$$
  - Explanation: Adding an effective exceptional divisor leaves a one-dimensional section space on the Iitaka fibre.
- **claim-exceptional-twist-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-exceptional-contraction)): Let $\pi:Z\to X$, $g:Z\to Y$ be a smooth equivariant resolution of a dominant $T$-invariant rational map to a smooth projective $Y$, with $\dim Y>0$ and geometrically integral generic fibre $F$ over $K=\mathbb C(Y)$. Suppose that $H^0(F,a\pi^*L\|_F)^T\ne0$ for some $a>0$, and that $$\dim_K H^0(F,(b\pi^*L+A)\|_F)^T\leq1$$ for every integer $b\geq0$ and every effective invariant $\pi$-exceptional integral divisor $A$. Then either a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section on $X$, or a dominant rational map $Y\dashrightarrow Y_2$ to a smooth projective base of smaller dimension preserves both the generic section and this bound for every exceptional twist on a smooth equivariant resolution of the composite map.
  - Explanation: Generic section and the rank bound for every exceptional twist persist on a smaller base.
- **claim-rigid-fibre-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-toroidal-contraction)): Let $\pi:Z\to X$, $g:Z\to Y$ be a smooth equivariant resolution of a dominant $T$-invariant rational map to a smooth projective $Y$, with $\dim Y>0$ and geometrically integral generic fibre $F$ over $K=\mathbb C(Y)$. Suppose there is a nonzero invariant section of $a\pi^*L\|_F$, $a>0$, and $$\dim_K H^0(F,(K_{Z/X}+ma\pi^*L)\|_F)^T=1 \qquad(m\geq0).$$ Then either a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section on $X$, or a dominant rational map $Y\dashrightarrow Y_2$ to a smooth projective base of smaller dimension preserves the generic section and these Jacobian-twisted ranks, with a possibly different positive integer $a$.
  - Explanation: Unweighted rigid-fibre variant preserving the section and Jacobian-twisted ranks.
- **claim-large-moment-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-eventual-contraction)): Suppose $K,s,d$ satisfy <a href="#g:bt:horizontal-regular" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:horizontal-regular">[g:bt:horizontal-regular]</a>–<a href="#g:bt:eventual-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:bt:eventual-rank">[g:bt:eventual-rank]</a>. If $\operatorname{trdeg}_{\mathbb C}K>0$, either a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section, or these same two conditions hold for some $K'\subset K$ with strictly smaller transcendence degree and a new invariant rational section of a positive multiple of $L$.
  - Explanation: Eventual rank one conditions descend to a subfield of smaller transcendence degree.
- **claim-original-prime-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-original-primes)): Let $K=\mathbb C(Y)\subseteq\mathbb C(X)^T$ be relatively algebraically closed, and let $s$ be an invariant rational section of $aL$, $a>0$, regular on a resolved generic fibre over $K$. Suppose that, for every $j\geq0$, $$\label{g:ba:original-rank} u\in\mathbb C(X)^T,\quad s^ju\text{ has no poles on primes of $X$ horizontal over $K$} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad u\in K.$$ Then either a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section, or a contraction to a smaller base preserves these two conditions.
  - Explanation: Uniqueness tested only at original primes with signed boundary gives a contraction.
- **claim-toric-generic-section** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-ba-toric-section)): For the rational quotient $K=\mathbb C(X)^T$, a positive multiple of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section on the smooth resolved generic fibre. The data satisfy <a href="#g:ba:original-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:ba:original-rank">[g:ba:original-rank]</a>.
  - Explanation: The rational quotient has an invariant generic section satisfying the original-prime condition.
- **claim-multiplicative-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-multiplicative-contraction)): Let $X$ be smooth projective and rationally connected, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act with the natural linearization. Let $\pi:Z\to X$, $g:Z\to Y$ resolve a dominant invariant rational map, with $Y$ smooth projective of positive dimension and $\mathbb C(Y)$ relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$. Write $E=K_{Z/X}$. Suppose a nonzero invariant rational section $s$ of $\ell\pi^*L$, $\ell>0$, and rational divisors $N$ on $Y$ and $D$ on $Z$ satisfy $$\ell^{-1}\operatorname{div}_Z(s)=g^*N+D,\qquad D\geq0.$$ Assume that $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. Suppose there is a rational simple normal crossings divisor $\Delta=\Delta^+-\Delta^-$ on $Y$, with every coefficient of $\Delta^+$ less than one, such that $-K_Y-\Delta$ is nef. Let $B$ be a reduced effective divisor on $Y$ such that* 1. *$\operatorname{Supp}\Delta^-\subset\operatorname{Supp}B$;* 2. *$\pi_*(D-tg^*B)\geq0$ for every sufficiently small rational $t>0$;* 3. *above every base prime outside $B$, some prime dominating it has coefficient zero in both $D$ and $E$.* *Finally suppose the invariant generic spaces of $E+k\pi^*L$ have rank one for $k=0$ and every positive multiple of one fixed integer. Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or a rational map $Y\dashrightarrow Y'$ with $\dim Y'<\dim Y$ gives a composite fibration retaining a nonzero invariant generic section in a positive multiple of $L$ and invariant adjoint rank one at zero and in every sufficiently divisible positive degree.
  - Explanation: Multiplicatively closed cotangent-twisted systems give a rank-preserving contraction without fixing a movable class.
- **claim-flat-section-line** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-flat-section-line)): Let $g:W\to T$ be a projective flat morphism with geometrically reduced fibers, where $T$ is a smooth integral projective variety. Let $P$ be a line bundle on $W$, and let $0\ne\tau\in H^0(W_{\eta},P_{\eta})$, where $\eta=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C(T)$. After a smooth projective modification of $T$, there are a line bundle $J$, a rational section $\eta_J$ of $J$, and a homomorphism $$a:g^*J\longrightarrow P$$ such that $a(g^*\eta_J)=\tau$ generically and $a\|_{W_t}\ne0$ for every $t\in T$.
  - Explanation: A generic section extends to a line and homomorphism nonzero on every fibre of a reduced flat family.
- **claim-flat-supremum-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-g-flat-supremum)): In the situation of Lemma <a href="#g:flat-section-line" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:flat-section-line">44</a>, give $J$ and $P$ continuous positive Hermitian metrics. For $a:g^*J\to P$, put $$c(t)=\sup_{w\in W_t}\|a(w)\|^2.$$ There are constants $0<c_-\le c_+<\infty$ with $c_-\le c(t)\le c_+$ for all $t\in T$.
  - Explanation: Fibre supremum norm is bounded above and below by positive constants.
- **claim-reduced-flat-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-g-reduced-flat-transfer)): Let $X,V,Y$ be smooth integral projective varieties, let $\pi:V\to X$ be birational, and let $f:V\to Y$ be surjective with connected fibers. Let $D$ be a reduced effective divisor on $X$, let $c\ge0$ be an integer, and let $L$ be a line bundle. Suppose that $L$ has a semipositive metric $h_L$ with locally bounded weights and that $M=-K_X+cD$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_M$. In local coordinates and a compatible frame of $M$, write $$d\mu=\|d\|^{2c}e^{-\varphi_M}\,dV_X,$$ where $d$ is an equation of $D$, and assume $\mu(X)<\infty$. Put $E=K_{V/X}$. Assume that for some integer $a>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\tau$ of $a\pi^*L$, regular over a dense base open subset, and that $$\operatorname{rk} f_*\mathcal O_V\bigl(E+c\pi^*D+pa\pi^*L\bigr)=1 \qquad\text{for every integer }p\ge0. \label{g:flat-adjoint-ranks}$$ If $\dim Y>0$, there are a smooth projective modification $Y'\to Y$, a line bundle $B$ on $Y'$, a reduced effective divisor $D'$, and integers $d_0>0$, $c'\ge0$ with the following properties. The line $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. The line $-K_{Y'}+c'D'$ has a semipositive metric inducing a finite measure. On a smooth resolution $\pi':V'\to X$, $f':V'\to Y'$ of the main transform there is a nonzero regular homomorphism $$(f')^*B\longrightarrow d_0a(\pi')^*L, \label{g:flat-output-map}$$ and $$\operatorname{Supp}\bigl(\pi'_* (f')^*D'\bigr) \subseteq\operatorname{Supp}D. \label{g:flat-pole-support}$$ Consequently, for every $m>0$, a nonzero rational section of $mB$ with poles only on $D'$ gives a nonzero rational section of $md_0aL$ with poles only on $D$.* *There is also a smooth invariant version. In that version $D=0$, both metrics are smooth, an algebraic torus acts compatibly on $X,V$ and the line bundles, its action on $Y$ is trivial, and $\tau$ is invariant. A Zariski dense compact subgroup preserves the metrics. Replace the ranks in <a href="#g:flat-adjoint-ranks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:flat-adjoint-ranks">[g:flat-adjoint-ranks]</a> by the ranks of the invariant parts, still for every $p\ge0$. Then the transferred section on $X$ is invariant and regular. Anticanonical bundles in this version carry their natural linearizations.
  - Explanation: Alteration and finite flat norm return a bounded line and finite-measure line to a modification of the original base.
- **claim-foliation-floor-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-reduced-flat-slope)): Let $X$ be smooth projective, let $D$ be a reduced effective divisor, and let $c\ge0$ be an integer. Suppose that $M=-K_X+cD$ has a semipositive singular metric with finite induced measure $\|d\|^{2c}e^{-\varphi}\,dV_X$. Every algebraically integrable saturated foliation $\mathcal F\subset T_X$ of positive rank and corank satisfies $$K_{\mathcal F}+M \quad\hbox{is pseudoeffective}. \label{g:flat-foliation-psef}$$
  - Explanation: Floor approximation makes the foliation determinant plus the log-anticanonical line pseudoeffective.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-rb-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:transfer\" label=\"g:transfer\"></span><span id=\"g:transfer-recalled\" label=\"g:transfer-recalled\"></span>Let $V$ be smooth connected projective, let $D\\geq0$ be integral, and suppose $A=-K_V+D$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_A$ with $\\int_V|s_D|_{h_A}^2<\\infty$. Let $L$ have a semipositive metric $h_L$ with bounded weights. Use the model diagram above with $0<\\dim Y\\leq\\dim V$. Suppose a nonzero rational section $\\rho$ of $kL$, $k>0$, has no horizontal poles on $\\bar V$. On a dense base open set put $$g_0(y)\\,d\\lambda_y=f_*p^*\\mu,\n \\qquad\n S_\\rho(y)=\\operatorname*{ess\\,sup}_{W_y}|p^*\\rho|_{h_L^k}^2.$$ Assume $-\\log g_0$ in coordinate frames and $-\\log S_\\rho$ have psh representatives on this open set. Then, after clearing a positive integer denominator, there are a line bundle $B$ with bounded semipositive weights, an effective integral divisor $D_Y$, and a regular nonzero map $$f^*B\\longrightarrow bk\\,p^*L$$ for some $b>0$. The line bundle $-K_Y+D_Y$ has a semipositive metric whose distinguished adjoint measure has finite mass. Every nonzero section of $aB+cD_Y$, where $a>0$ and $c\\geq0$ are integers, produces a nonzero rational section of $abkL$ with poles only on $D$. If the data and $\\rho$ are invariant for a torus acting trivially on $Y$, then the produced section is invariant.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },

        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:moments\" label=\"g:moments\"></span>In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#rb:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rb:transfer\">4</a>, suppose on the generic fibre of $f$ that $$h^0\\bigl(W_\\eta,(J+p^*D+mkp^*L)|_{W_\\eta}\\bigr)=1\n                       \\qquad(m\\geq0).$$ Then the two psh assumptions in that lemma hold.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rb-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:determinant\" label=\"g:determinant\"></span>Let $L$ have bounded semipositive weights on a smooth projective $n$-fold $V$ with $H^0(V,\\Omega_V^j)=0$ for $j>0$.         "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-068.5"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-contraction-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety with $H^0(X,\\Omega_X^q)=0$ for $q>0$, and suppose that $L=-K_X$ has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$, with its natural action on $L$. Let $f:X\\dashrightarrow Z$ be a dominant $T$-invariant rational map to a smooth connected projective variety, where $\\mathbb C(Z)$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$. On a smooth projective resolution $\\pi:Y\\to X$, $f_Y:Y\\to Z$, put $L_Y=\\pi^*L$, $E_Y=K_{Y/X}$, and $\\eta=\\operatorname{Spec}\\mathbb C(Z)$. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$H^0(Y_\\eta,pL_Y|_{Y_\\eta})^T\\ne0$ for some integer $p>0$;*\n\n2.  *there is an integer $a>0$ such that $$\\dim_{\\mathbb C(Z)}H^0(Y_\\eta,(E_Y+mL_Y)|_{Y_\\eta})^T=1\n    \\quad\\text{for every positive multiple }m\\text{ of }a.$$*\n\n*Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or there is a dominant rational map $h:Z\\dashrightarrow W$ to a smooth connected projective variety with $\\dim W<\\dim Z$ such that $h\\circ f$ satisfies the same two conditions, possibly with different positive integers. The field $\\mathbb C(W)$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-contraction-theorem",

        "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-density-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For the prepared toroidal diagram and smooth positive volume above, near an arbitrary base point, with $y_i=0$ the local boundary branches, for every $\\eta>0$, $$\\label{g:cf:upper-density}\n \\rho(y)\\leq C_\\eta\n       \\prod_i|y_i|^{-2(1-c_i+\\eta)}$$ on the general smooth locus.         "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-metric-conclusions",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding setup, $V$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights.         "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:bt:weighted-slope\" label=\"g:bt:weighted-slope\"></span><span id=\"g:slope-recalled\" label=\"g:slope-recalled\"></span>Let $Y$ be smooth projective and $P\\geq0$ rational. If $\\dim Y>0$ and $\\alpha\\ne0$ is movable, then $\\mu_{\\alpha,\\min}(T_Y)\\geq-P\\cdot\\alpha$. In particular, $P\\cdot\\alpha=0$ gives $\\mu_{\\alpha,\\min}(T_Y)\\geq0$ and $c_1(M)\\cdot\\alpha\\leq0$.",

        "exact_quote": "Use the smooth metric and action above. Let $p:W\\to X$ be birational with $W$ normal, and let $f:W\\to Y$ be projective, equidimensional and toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings, with $Y$ smooth. Include the exceptional locus of $p$ in the source boundary. Suppose that an invariant rational section $s$ of $aL$, $a>0$, is regular on the generic fibre and that on a smooth equivariant resolved diagram $$\\label{g:bt:zeroth-rank}\n \\dim_K H^0\\bigl(F,(K_{Z/X}+ma\\pi^*L)|_F\\bigr)^T=1\n                           \\qquad(m\\geq0).$$ Normalize $s$ by vertical minima as in <a href=\"#g:bt:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"g:bt:normalization\">[g:bt:normalization]</a>. For a boundary prime $Q$ put $$A_E=1+\\operatorname{ord}_E K_{W/X},\\qquad\n c_Q=\\min_{E\\mapsto Q}\\frac{A_E}{e_E},\\qquad\n \\Theta=\\sum_Q\\max\\{c_Q-1,0\\}Q.$$ Then $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights, and $-K_Y+\\Theta$ has a semipositive metric with locally integrable squared frame norms. The pullback $f^*\\Theta$ is exceptional over $X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-bt-signed-slope",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth projective and let $B$ be a rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients less than one. Write $B=B^+-B^-$ in disjoint positive and negative parts, and suppose that $-(K_Y+B)$ is nef. Then $$hB^- -c_1(M)\n                         \\quad\\hbox{is pseudoeffective}$$ for every line subsheaf $M\\subseteq(\\Omega_Y^1)^{\\otimes h}$, $h\\geq0$. If $\\dim Y>0$ and $\\alpha\\ne0$ is a movable class with $\\alpha\\cdot B^-=0$, this gives $\\mu_{\\alpha,\\min}(T_Y)\\geq0$ and $c_1(M)\\cdot\\alpha\\leq0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rb-allowed",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:allowed\" label=\"g:allowed\"></span>Let $V$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\\mathbb C$ with $H^0(V,\\Omega_V^j)=0$ for every $j>0$. Let $D\\geq0$ be integral and suppose $-K_V+D$ carries a semipositive singular metric whose measure $|s_D|^2$ has finite mass. If a line bundle $L$ carries a semipositive metric with bounded weights, then $$H^0(V,aL+cD)\\ne0$$ for some integers $a>0$ and $c\\geq0$.       },

        "exact_quote": "The conclusion of Theorem <a href=\"#rb:allowed\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rb:allowed\">23</a> on a rationally connected $V$ follows by dimension induction through rationally connected bases, using fibre spaces that satisfy the following two conditions: for an ample divisor $H$ on the base, $$aL+cD-p_*f^*H\\ \\hbox{is pseudoeffective for some }a>0,c\\geq0,$$ and a positive multiple of $p^*L$ has a nonzero section on the generic fibre without additional $D$-poles.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rb-prekopa",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:prekopa\" label=\"g:prekopa\"></span>Let $r\\geq0$ be an integer, let $U$ be a complex manifold, and let $\\Phi(z,x)$ be a smooth function on $U\\times\\mathbb R^r$ such that $\\Phi(z,\\operatorname{Re}w)$ is plurisubharmonic on $U\\times\\mathbb C^r$. Suppose $$0<\\int_{\\mathbb R^r}e^{-\\Phi(z,x)}\\,dx<\\infty$$ for almost every $z$. Then the almost-everywhere functions $$-\\log\\int_{\\mathbb R^r}e^{-\\Phi(z,x)}\\,dx,\n \\qquad\n -\\log\\sup_{x\\in\\mathbb R^r}e^{-\\Phi(z,x)}$$ have psh representatives, whenever the second expression is taken on the locus where its supremum is finite and positive. Under the stated integrability assumption that supremum is finite and positive for almost every $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rb-prekopa",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"g:prekopa\" label=\"g:prekopa\"></span>Let $r\\geq0$ be an integer, let $U$ be a complex manifold, and let $\\Phi(z,x)$ be a smooth function on $U\\times\\mathbb R^r$ such that $\\Phi(z,\\operatorname{Re}w)$ is plurisubharmonic on $U\\times\\mathbb C^r$. Then the almost-everywhere functions $$-\\log\\int_{\\mathbb R^r}e^{-\\Phi(z,x)}\\,dx,\n \\qquad\n -\\log\\sup_{x\\in\\mathbb R^r}e^{-\\Phi(z,x)}$$ have psh representatives, whenever the second expression is taken on the locus where its supremum is finite and positive. Under the stated integrability assumption that supremum is finite and positive for almost every $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.5"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-uniqueness",
        "exact_quote": "With these data, the following statements are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *For every integer $j\\geq0$ and every nonzero invariant rational section $u$ of $jaL$ with no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$, one has $u/s^j\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For every integer $b\\geq0$, the ratio of any two nonzero invariant rational sections of $bL$ having no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$ belongs to $K$.*\n\n*Both statements include degree zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-uniqueness",
          "exact_quote": "With these data, the following statements are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *For every integer $j\\geq0$ and every nonzero invariant rational section $u$ of $jaL$ with no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$, one has $u/s^j\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For every integer $b\\geq0$, the ratio of any two nonzero invariant rational sections of $bL$ having no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$ belongs to $K$.*\n\n*Both statements include degree zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With these data, the following statements are equivalent.*\n\n1.  *For every integer $j\\geq0$ and every nonzero invariant rational section $u$ of $jaL$ with no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$, one has $u/s^j\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *For every integer $b\\geq0$, the ratio of any two nonzero invariant rational sections of $bL$ having no poles at the $K$-horizontal primes of $X$ belongs to $K$.*\n\n*Both statements include degree zero.",
      "explanation": "Two uniqueness conditions tested at original horizontal primes are equivalent.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-null-field",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $d>0$ and let $\\alpha\\ne0$ be a movable numerical curve class. Call a prime divisor $P$ *null* if $\\alpha\\cdot P=0$, and set $$K_\\alpha=\\{0\\}\\cup\\{u\\in\\mathbb C(Y)^*:\n          \\operatorname{Supp}(\\operatorname{div}u)\n          \\text{ consists of null primes}\\}.$$ Then $K_\\alpha$ is relatively algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(Y)$ and has transcendence degree less than $d$. It is the field of the Stein base of any linear system of degree zero against $\\alpha$ having maximal image dimension among all such systems. Every prime of $Y$ vertical over $K_\\alpha$ is null. If $\\operatorname{Pic}(Y)$ is finitely generated, only finitely many null primes are horizontal over $K_\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-null-field",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $d>0$ and let $\\alpha\\ne0$ be a movable numerical curve class. It is the field of the Stein base of any linear system of degree zero against $\\alpha$ having maximal image dimension among all such systems. Every prime of $Y$ vertical over $K_\\alpha$ is null. If $\\operatorname{Pic}(Y)$ is finitely generated, only finitely many null primes are horizontal over $K_\\alpha$.",

        }

      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-supporting-class",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the data of Section <a href=\"#g:cf:minima\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"g:cf:minima\">8.3</a> and suppose no positive power of $L$ has a nonzero invariant global section. Then there is a nonzero movable class $\\alpha$ on $Y$ satisfying $$\\alpha\\cdot D=\\alpha\\cdot D^*=0,\n \\qquad \\alpha\\cdot\\Gamma=0\n             \\quad\\text{for every omitted prime }\\Gamma.$$ In particular, $d^*_\\Gamma=d_\\Gamma$ whenever $\\alpha\\cdot\\Gamma>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-supporting-class",
          "exact_quote": "Assume the data of Section <a href=\"#g:cf:minima\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"g:cf:minima\">8.3</a> and suppose no positive power of $L$ has a nonzero invariant global section.         "anchor": "stmt-g-cf-evaluation-slope",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. Suppose that $f:X\\dashrightarrow Y$ is dominant and invariant and that every invariant rational function on $X$ having no poles at the $f$-horizontal prime divisors of $X$ belongs to $\\mathbb C(Y)$. Choose a normal projective graph model $$X\\xleftarrow{p}W\\xrightarrow{\\varphi}Y$$ on which every vertical prime maps onto a prime divisor of $Y$. Let $N$ be the reduced sum of the primes of $Y$ above which all prime divisors of $W$ are exceptional over $X$.*\n\n*For every dominant rational map $g:Y\\dashrightarrow U$ to a smooth projective variety, let $\\mathcal F\\subset T_Y$ be the saturated sheaf tangent to its general fibers. There is an integer $c\\geq0$ such that $$\\det(T_Y/\\mathcal F)+cN\n \\quad\\text{is pseudoeffective}.$$ Consequently, if $\\alpha\\ne0$ is a movable curve class on $Y$ with $\\alpha\\cdot N=0$, then every line subsheaf $A\\subset(\\Omega_Y^1)^{\\otimes j}$, $j\\geq0$, satisfies $\\alpha\\cdot c_1(A)\\leq0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Keep the fixed-section data of Section <a href=\"#g:cf:minima\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"g:cf:minima\">8.3</a> and assume $H^0(Y,\\Omega_Y^q)=0$ for $q>0$. After taking a power of $s$, let $D,D^*$ be integral, put $G=D^*-D\\geq0$, and let $N$ be the reduced omitted-prime divisor. Suppose $D$ has a bounded semipositive metric and that every cotangent line has nonpositive degree against every movable class annihilating $N$. If there is no invariant global section of a positive multiple of $L$, a smaller base preserving an invariant generic section of a positive multiple of $L$ and the original-prime uniqueness condition is obtained from one of the systems $$|mD+iM+kG+lN|,\\qquad m,i,k,l\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0},$$ where $M$ is a single cotangent determinant line and $H^0(Y,M+m_jD)\\ne0$ for unbounded positive $m_j$.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:Z\\to X$, $g:Z\\to Y$ be a smooth equivariant resolution of a dominant $T$-invariant rational map to a smooth projective $Y$, with $\\dim Y>0$ and geometrically integral generic fibre $F$ over $K=\\mathbb C(Y)$.         "anchor": "stmt-g-multiplicative-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be smooth projective and rationally connected, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act with the natural linearization. Let $\\pi:Z\\to X$, $g:Z\\to Y$ resolve a dominant invariant rational map, with $Y$ smooth projective of positive dimension and $\\mathbb C(Y)$ relatively algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$. Write $E=K_{Z/X}$. Suppose a nonzero invariant rational section $s$ of $\\ell\\pi^*L$, $\\ell>0$, and rational divisors $N$ on $Y$ and $D$ on $Z$ satisfy $$\\ell^{-1}\\operatorname{div}_Z(s)=g^*N+D,\\qquad D\\geq0.$$ Assume that $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. Suppose there is a rational simple normal crossings divisor $\\Delta=\\Delta^+-\\Delta^-$ on $Y$, with every coefficient of $\\Delta^+$ less than one, such that $-K_Y-\\Delta$ is nef. Let $B$ be a reduced effective divisor on $Y$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\operatorname{Supp}\\Delta^-\\subset\\operatorname{Supp}B$;*\n\n2.  *$\\pi_*(D-tg^*B)\\geq0$ for every sufficiently small rational $t>0$;*\n\n3.  *above every base prime outside $B$, some prime dominating it has coefficient zero in both $D$ and $E$.*\n\n*Finally suppose the invariant generic spaces of $E+k\\pi^*L$ have rank one for $k=0$ and every positive multiple of one fixed integer. Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or a rational map $Y\\dashrightarrow Y'$ with $\\dim Y'<\\dim Y$ gives a composite fibration retaining a nonzero invariant generic section in a positive multiple of $L$ and invariant adjoint rank one at zero and in every sufficiently divisible positive degree.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-multiplicative-contraction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be smooth projective and rationally connected, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act with the natural linearization. Let $\\pi:Z\\to X$, $g:Z\\to Y$ resolve a dominant invariant rational map, with $Y$ smooth projective of positive dimension and $\\mathbb C(Y)$ relatively algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$. Write $E=K_{Z/X}$. Suppose a nonzero invariant rational section $s$ of $\\ell\\pi^*L$, $\\ell>0$, and rational divisors $N$ on $Y$ and $D$ on $Z$ satisfy $$\\ell^{-1}\\operatorname{div}_Z(s)=g^*N+D,\\qquad D\\geq0.$$ Assume that $N$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. Suppose there is a rational simple normal crossings divisor $\\Delta=\\Delta^+-\\Delta^-$ on $Y$, with every coefficient of $\\Delta^+$ less than one, such that $-K_Y-\\Delta$ is nef. Let $B$ be a reduced effective divisor on $Y$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\operatorname{Supp}\\Delta^-\\subset\\operatorname{Supp}B$;*\n\n2.  *$\\pi_*(D-tg^*B)\\geq0$ for every sufficiently small rational $t>0$;*\n\n3.  *above every base prime outside $B$, some prime dominating it has coefficient zero in both $D$ and $E$.*\n\n*Finally suppose the invariant generic spaces of $E+k\\pi^*L$ have rank one for $k=0$ and every positive multiple of one fixed integer. Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or a rational map $Y\\dashrightarrow Y'$ with $\\dim Y'<\\dim Y$ gives a composite fibration retaining a nonzero invariant generic section in a positive multiple of $L$ and invariant adjoint rank one at zero and in every sufficiently divisible positive degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.5"

      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be smooth projective and rationally connected, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act with the natural linearization. Suppose there is a rational simple normal crossings divisor $\\Delta=\\Delta^+-\\Delta^-$ on $Y$, with every coefficient of $\\Delta^+$ less than one, such that $-K_Y-\\Delta$ is nef. Let $B$ be a reduced effective divisor on $Y$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\operatorname{Supp}\\Delta^-\\subset\\operatorname{Supp}B$;*\n\n2.  *$\\pi_*(D-tg^*B)\\geq0$ for every sufficiently small rational $t>0$;*\n\n3.  *above every base prime outside $B$, some prime dominating it has coefficient zero in both $D$ and $E$.*\n\n*Finally suppose the invariant generic spaces of $E+k\\pi^*L$ have rank one for $k=0$ and every positive multiple of one fixed integer. Then either $H^0(X,rL)^T\\ne0$ for some $r>0$, or a rational map $Y\\dashrightarrow Y'$ with $\\dim Y'<\\dim Y$ gives a composite fibration retaining a nonzero invariant generic section in a positive multiple of $L$ and invariant adjoint rank one at zero and in every sufficiently divisible positive degree.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-flat-section-line",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g:W\\to T$ be a projective flat morphism with geometrically reduced fibers, where $T$ is a smooth integral projective variety. Let $P$ be a line bundle on $W$, and let $0\\ne\\tau\\in H^0(W_{\\eta},P_{\\eta})$, where $\\eta=\\operatorname{Spec}\\mathbb C(T)$. After a smooth projective modification of $T$, there are a line bundle $J$, a rational section $\\eta_J$ of $J$, and a homomorphism $$a:g^*J\\longrightarrow P$$ such that $a(g^*\\eta_J)=\\tau$ generically and $a|_{W_t}\\ne0$ for every $t\\in T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-reduced-flat-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,V,Y$ be smooth integral projective varieties, let $\\pi:V\\to X$ be birational, and let $f:V\\to Y$ be surjective with connected fibers. Let $D$ be a reduced effective divisor on $X$, let $c\\ge0$ be an integer, and let $L$ be a line bundle. Suppose that $L$ has a semipositive metric $h_L$ with locally bounded weights and that $M=-K_X+cD$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_M$. In local coordinates and a compatible frame of $M$, write $$d\\mu=|d|^{2c}e^{-\\varphi_M}\\,dV_X,$$ where $d$ is an equation of $D$, and assume $\\mu(X)<\\infty$. Put $E=K_{V/X}$. Assume that for some integer $a>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\tau$ of $a\\pi^*L$, regular over a dense base open subset, and that $$\\operatorname{rk} f_*\\mathcal O_V\\bigl(E+c\\pi^*D+pa\\pi^*L\\bigr)=1\n                \\qquad\\text{for every integer }p\\ge0.\n \\label{g:flat-adjoint-ranks}$$ If $\\dim Y>0$, there are a smooth projective modification $Y'\\to Y$, a line bundle $B$ on $Y'$, a reduced effective divisor $D'$, and integers $d_0>0$, $c'\\ge0$ with the following properties. The line $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. The line $-K_{Y'}+c'D'$ has a semipositive metric inducing a finite measure. On a smooth resolution $\\pi':V'\\to X$, $f':V'\\to Y'$ of the main transform there is a nonzero regular homomorphism $$(f')^*B\\longrightarrow d_0a(\\pi')^*L,\n \\label{g:flat-output-map}$$ and $$\\operatorname{Supp}\\bigl(\\pi'_* (f')^*D'\\bigr)\n                            \\subseteq\\operatorname{Supp}D.\n \\label{g:flat-pole-support}$$ Consequently, for every $m>0$, a nonzero rational section of $mB$ with poles only on $D'$ gives a nonzero rational section of $md_0aL$ with poles only on $D$.*\n\n*There is also a smooth invariant version. In that version $D=0$, both metrics are smooth, an algebraic torus acts compatibly on $X,V$ and the line bundles, its action on $Y$ is trivial, and $\\tau$ is invariant. A Zariski dense compact subgroup preserves the metrics. Replace the ranks in <a href=\"#g:flat-adjoint-ranks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"g:flat-adjoint-ranks\">[g:flat-adjoint-ranks]</a> by the ranks of the invariant parts, still for every $p\\ge0$. Then the transferred section on $X$ is invariant and regular. Anticanonical bundles in this version carry their natural linearizations.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-g-reduced-flat-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,V,Y$ be smooth integral projective varieties, let $\\pi:V\\to X$ be birational, and let $f:V\\to Y$ be surjective with connected fibers. Let $D$ be a reduced effective divisor on $X$, let $c\\ge0$ be an integer, and let $L$ be a line bundle. Suppose that $L$ has a semipositive metric $h_L$ with locally bounded weights and that $M=-K_X+cD$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_M$. In local coordinates and a compatible frame of $M$, write $$d\\mu=|d|^{2c}e^{-\\varphi_M}\\,dV_X,$$ where $d$ is an equation of $D$, and assume $\\mu(X)<\\infty$. Put $E=K_{V/X}$. Assume that for some integer $a>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\tau$ of $a\\pi^*L$, regular over a dense base open subset, and that $$\\operatorname{rk} f_*\\mathcal O_V\\bigl(E+c\\pi^*D+pa\\pi^*L\\bigr)=1\n                \\qquad\\text{for every integer }p\\ge0.\n \\label{g:flat-adjoint-ranks}$$ If $\\dim Y>0$, there are a smooth projective modification $Y'\\to Y$, a line bundle $B$ on $Y'$, a reduced effective divisor $D'$, and integers $d_0>0$, $c'\\ge0$ with the following properties. The line $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. The line $-K_{Y'}+c'D'$ has a semipositive metric inducing a finite measure. On a smooth resolution $\\pi':V'\\to X$, $f':V'\\to Y'$ of the main transform there is a nonzero regular homomorphism $$(f')^*B\\longrightarrow d_0a(\\pi')^*L,\n \\label{g:flat-output-map}$$ and $$\\operatorname{Supp}\\bigl(\\pi'_* (f')^*D'\\bigr)\n                            \\subseteq\\operatorname{Supp}D.\n \\label{g:flat-pole-support}$$ Consequently, for every $m>0$, a nonzero rational section of $mB$ with poles only on $D'$ gives a nonzero rational section of $md_0aL$ with poles only on $D$.*\n\n*There is also a smooth invariant version. In that version $D=0$, both metrics are smooth, an algebraic torus acts compatibly on $X,V$ and the line bundles, its action on $Y$ is trivial, and $\\tau$ is invariant. A Zariski dense compact subgroup preserves the metrics. Replace the ranks in <a href=\"#g:flat-adjoint-ranks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"g:flat-adjoint-ranks\">[g:flat-adjoint-ranks]</a> by the ranks of the invariant parts, still for every $p\\ge0$. Then the transferred section on $X$ is invariant and regular. Anticanonical bundles in this version carry their natural linearizations.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $X,V,Y$ be smooth integral projective varieties, let $\\pi:V\\to X$ be birational, and let $f:V\\to Y$ be surjective with connected fibers. Let $D$ be a reduced effective divisor on $X$, let $c\\ge0$ be an integer, and let $L$ be a line bundle. Suppose that $L$ has a semipositive metric $h_L$ with locally bounded weights and that $M=-K_X+cD$ has a semipositive singular metric $h_M$. In local coordinates and a compatible frame of $M$, write $$d\\mu=|d|^{2c}e^{-\\varphi_M}\\,dV_X,$$ where $d$ is an equation of $D$, and assume $\\mu(X)<\\infty$. Put $E=K_{V/X}$. Assume that for some integer $a>0$ there is a nonzero rational section $\\tau$ of $a\\pi^*L$, regular over a dense base open subset, and that $$\\operatorname{rk} f_*\\mathcal O_V\\bigl(E+c\\pi^*D+pa\\pi^*L\\bigr)=1\n                \\qquad\\text{for every integer }p\\ge0.\n \\label{g:flat-adjoint-ranks}$$ If $\\dim Y>0$, there are a smooth projective modification $Y'\\to Y$, a line bundle $B$ on $Y'$, a reduced effective divisor $D'$, and integers $d_0>0$, $c'\\ge0$ with the following properties. The line $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. The line $-K_{Y'}+c'D'$ has a semipositive metric inducing a finite measure. On a smooth resolution $\\pi':V'\\to X$, $f':V'\\to Y'$ of the main transform there is a nonzero regular homomorphism $$(f')^*B\\longrightarrow d_0a(\\pi')^*L,\n \\label{g:flat-output-map}$$ and $$\\operatorname{Supp}\\bigl(\\pi'_* (f')^*D'\\bigr)\n                            \\subseteq\\operatorname{Supp}D.\n \\label{g:flat-pole-support}$$ Consequently, for every $m>0$, a nonzero rational section of $mB$ with poles only on $D'$ gives a nonzero rational section of $md_0aL$ with poles only on $D$.*\n\n*There is also a smooth invariant version. In that version $D=0$, both metrics are smooth, an algebraic torus acts compatibly on $X,V$ and the line bundles, its action on $Y$ is trivial, and $\\tau$ is invariant. A Zariski dense compact subgroup preserves the metrics. Replace the ranks in <a href=\"#g:flat-adjoint-ranks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"g:flat-adjoint-ranks\">[g:flat-adjoint-ranks]</a> by the ranks of the invariant parts, still for every $p\\ge0$. Then the transferred section on $X$ is invariant and regular. Anticanonical bundles in this version carry their natural linearizations.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **anticanonical bundle** ([source](#g:introduction)): On a smooth variety $X$, the anticanonical bundle is $-K_X=\det T_X$, with its natural linearization whenever a group acts.
- **semipositive singular Hermitian metric** ([source](#g:introduction)): A singular Hermitian metric is semipositive if its local squared frame norm is $e^{-\varphi}$ for a plurisubharmonic weight $\varphi$.
- **locally bounded weights** ([source](#g:introduction)): A metric with locally bounded weights satisfies two-sided local bounds on $\varphi$.
- **toroidal embedding** ([source](#g:sec-toroidal-volume)): A toroidal embedding is a normal variety with an open subset locally modeled on a toric variety and its dense torus.
- **sub-klt pair** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The pair is *sub-klt* if every coefficient of $\Theta_{W'}$ is less than one on every such model; negative coefficients are allowed.
- **section condition and rank condition** ([source](#g:cf:generic-conditions)): We call its two hypotheses the *section condition* and the *rank condition*.
- **horizontal valuation over K** ([source](#g:cf:conditions)): A divisorial valuation is *horizontal over $K$* when it is zero on $K^*$; on a proper model mapping to a projective model of $K$, this means that its center dominates the base.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-volume-subklt** [claim](#stmt-g-volume-subklt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-volume-subklt): Compare log discrepancies with the full toroidal boundary. -> sub-klt boundary with exceptional pullback
- **s-toroidal-volume** [claim](#stmt-g-toroidal-volume) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Fibre integration, ramification lower bound, psh extension and projection formula integrability. -> unweighted integrable metric on the corrected line
- **s-iitaka-zero** [claim](#stmt-g-correction-iitaka-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-correction-iitaka-zero): Exceptional poles force constant rational functions. -> one-dimensional section space
- **s-rank-one** [claim](#stmt-rb-rank-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-rank-one): Direct-image positivity and power-mean limits. -> psh moment weights
- **s-transfer** [claim](#stmt-rb-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-transfer): Vertical-minimum normalization and psh extension. -> bounded metric and finite-volume base metric
- **s-determinant** [claim](#stmt-rb-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-determinant): Riemann–Roch, hard Lefschetz and determinant extraction. -> effective divisors in unbounded twists
- **s-density-bounds** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-density-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-density-bounds): Logarithmic coordinates and coarea estimates. -> two-sided density bounds
- **s-metric-conclusions** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-metric-conclusions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-metric-conclusions): Apply rank-one and transfer lemmas, extend weights, use Nadel vanishing. -> bounded metric and nef divisor
- **s-cotangent-slope** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-cotangent-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-cotangent-slope): Orbifold cotangent positivity on adapted covers. -> nonpositive cotangent degree
- **s-effective-sequence** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-effective-sequence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-effective-sequence): Determinant lemma plus cotangent slope. -> zero-degree effective divisors
- **s-maximal-field** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-maximal-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-maximal-field): Product systems and Segre embedding. -> ratios in the base function field
- **s-horizontal-slope** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-horizontal-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-horizontal-slope): Three-index affine interpolation of horizontal multiplicities. -> effectivity on the generic fibre
- **s-contraction** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-contraction-theorem) / [proof](#g:cf:rank-preservation): Pole clearing by base twists and the maximal-field property. -> smaller base with both conditions
- **s-weighted-approx** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-weighted-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-weighted-approximation): Nadel vanishing, point extension and Gaussian sections. -> sub-klt approximation
- **s-slope-principle** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-slope-principle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-slope-principle): Harder–Narasimhan slopes and algebraicity. -> tensor bounds
- **s-finite-volume-cotangent** [claim](#stmt-rb-cotangent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-cotangent): Approximation plus relative pseudoeffectivity. -> cotangent estimate
- **s-unweighted-slope** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-unweighted-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-unweighted-slope): Klt approximation and orbifold tensor positivity. -> movable slope bound
- **s-bounded-maximum** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-bounded-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-bounded-maximum): Power-mean limit and psh extension. -> bounded metric on normalized line
- **s-weighted-transfer** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-weighted-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-weighted-transfer): Moments, zeroth density and exceptional correction. -> two metrics and return of sections
- **s-toroidal-transfer** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-toroidal-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-toroidal-transfer): Toroidal density with discrepancy lemma. -> unweighted integrable metric
- **s-large-moment** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-large-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-large-moment): One large moment with discrepancy absorption. -> donor metric
- **s-signed-slope** [claim](#stmt-g-bt-signed-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-bt-signed-slope): Relative pseudoeffectivity with signed boundary. -> signed cotangent bound
- **s-allowed** [claim](#stmt-rb-allowed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-allowed): Maximal admissible systems, determinant divisors, interpolation and induction. -> nonzero section with allowed poles
- **s-invariant** [claim](#stmt-rb-invariant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-invariant): Rational quotient, wedge of action fields, invariant transfer. -> invariant anticanonical section
- **s-prekopa** [claim](#stmt-rb-prekopa) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rb-prekopa): Brascamp–Lieb variance and high-power limit. -> psh integral and supremum weights
- **s-null-field** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-null-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-null-field): Support conditions and big-divisor argument. -> null-prime field
- **s-supporting-class** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-supporting-class) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-supporting-class): Cone duality modulo omitted primes. -> annihilating movable class
- **s-dickson** [claim](#stmt-g-cf-dickson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-cf-dickson): Dickson's lemma on horizontal multiplicity vectors. -> new regular section
- **s-reduced-flat-transfer** [claim](#stmt-g-reduced-flat-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-reduced-flat-transfer): Alteration, finite flat norm, density lower bound. -> bounded line and finite-measure line on original base
- **s-foliation-floor** [claim](#stmt-g-reduced-flat-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-reduced-flat-slope): Multiplier-ideal approximation and floor bound. -> pseudoeffective foliation determinant

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-correction-coefficient-formula**: For each base prime Q the coefficient a_Q is the larger of zero and the minimum, over the primes E dominating Q, of (1+a_E)/e_E - 1, so it is a finite rational computed from the Jacobian orders a_E and pullback multiplicities e_E.
- **inv-correction-effective**: Every coefficient a_Q is nonnegative because of the clamp at zero, so A_0 is an effective rational divisor with finite support.
- **inv-correction-coefficient-bound**: When a_Q is positive, every prime E over Q satisfies e_E a_Q - a_E <= 1 - e_E <= 0, which is the coefficient bound used for the sub-klt property.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-correction-nonequidimensional-model**: Apply the coefficient formula to a morphism whose fibre dimensions jump or that is not toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings. -> Vertical primes may map into codimension two sets, so the minimum over primes dominating Q no longer controls all poles and the exceptional-support conclusion is lost.
- **anti-correction-drop-clamp**: Take the bare minimum of (1+a_E)/e_E - 1 without clamping at zero and read the result as an effective divisor. -> Negative coefficients appear, so the output is not effective and the exceptional-support argument does not apply.
- **anti-correction-code-certifies-metric**: Treat a successful run of the coefficient computation as evidence that the singular metric exists and is integrable. -> The computation never touches the metric, the curvature, or the adjoint rank; those are separate hypotheses.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. Only the explicit boundary correction of the toroidal volume theorem is a finite computation: given the finitely many pairs (a_E, e_E) over each base prime it returns the exact rational coefficients a_Q. The metric, curvature and section-existence parts of the paper are analytic and non-constructive and are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin: n, then per base prime: k followed by k integer pairs (a_E e_E); a_E is the Jacobian order, e_E the pullback multiplicity
# PRECONDITIONS: n>=1, every k>=1, a_E>=0, e_E>=1; the toroidal equidimensional model and the rank-one hypothesis are assumed, not checked
# OUTPUTS: one line per base prime with the coefficient a_Q as an integer or a reduced fraction p/q
# TIME: linear in the total number of pairs
# SPACE: linear in the number of base primes
# ARITHMETIC: exact rationals via Fraction
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0])
    pos = 1
    out = []
    for _ in range(n):
        k = int(data[pos])
        pos += 1
        best = None
        for _ in range(k):
            a = int(data[pos])
            e = int(data[pos + 1])
            pos += 2
            v = Fraction(1 + a, e) - 1
            if best is None or v < best:
                best = v
        aq = max(Fraction(0), best)
        out.append(str(aq))
    print("\n".join(out))


main()
```
